Function.evaldefBasic.lean:35
Evaluate a function at an argument. Useful if you want to talk about the partially applied Function.eval x : (∀ x, β x) → β x.
Lean
@[reducible, simp] def eval {β : α → Sort*} (x : α) (f : ∀ x, β x) : β x := f x
Dedukti
2 lines
def Function__eval : (x1 : Elem X) -> (f : ((x2 : Elem X) -> x x2)) -> Elem (x x1) :=
  x1 => f => f x1.
English
Let $x_{1} \in X$. Let $f$ be a function from elements $x_{2}$ of $X$ to elements of $x$ applied to $x_{2}$. Then the evaluation of $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$ is an element of $x$ applied to $x_{1}$ defined as the function that maps $f$ to $x_{2}$ applied to $x_{1}$ and $f$.
Formula
$x_{1} \in X , f \in X \rightarrow x (x_{2})\vdash \operatorname{eval} (x_{1} , x_{2} , f):= \lambda f . x_{2} (x_{1} , f)\in x (x_{1})$

English

Formula
$X \in \mathrm{Set} , x \in X \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} , x_{1} \in X , f \in X \rightarrow x (x_{2})\vdash \EqtMACRoAU {\operatorname{eval} (x_{1} , f)}{f (x_{1})}$

Function.hfunexttheoremBasic.lean:121
Lean
lemma hfunext {α α' : Sort u} {β : α → Sort v} {β' : α' → Sort v} {f : ∀ a, β a} {f' : ∀ a, β' a}
    (hα : α = α') (h : ∀ a a', a ≍ a' → f a ≍ f' a') : f ≍ f' := by
  subst hα
  have : ∀ a, f a ≍ f' a := fun a ↦ h a a (HEq.refl a)
  have : β = β' := by funext a; exact type_eq_of_heq (this a)
  subst this
  grind

lemma funext_iff_of_subsingleton [Subsingleton α] {g : α → β} (x y : α) :

theorem injective_comp_right_iff_surjective {γ : Type*} [Nontrivial γ] :
    Injective (fun g : β → γ ↦ g ∘ f) ↔ Surjective f := by
  refine ⟨not_imp_not.mp fun not_surj inj ↦ not_subsingleton γ ⟨fun c c' ↦ ?_⟩,
    (·.injective_comp_right)⟩
  have ⟨b₀, hb⟩ := not_forall.mp not_surj
  classical have := inj (a₁ := fun _ ↦ c) (a₂ := (if · = b₀ then c' else c)) ?_
  · simpa using congr_fun this b₀
  ext a; simp only [comp_apply, ite_eq_right fun h ↦ hb ⟨a, h⟩]

theorem Bijective.existsUnique_iff {f : α → β} (hf : Bijective f) {p : β → Prop} :
    (∃! y, p y) ↔ ∃! x, p (f x) :=
  ⟨fun ⟨y, hpy, hy⟩ ↦
    let ⟨x, hx⟩ := hf.surjective y
    ⟨x, by simpa [hx], fun z (hz : p (f z)) ↦ hf.injective <| hx.symm ▸ hy _ hz⟩,
    fun ⟨x, hpx, hx⟩ ↦
    ⟨f x, hpx, fun y hy ↦
      let ⟨z, hz⟩ := hf.surjective y
      hz ▸ congr_arg f (hx _ (by simpa [hz]))⟩⟩

Function.not_surjective_TypetheoremBasic.lean:386
There is no surjection from α : Type u into Type (max u v). This theorem demonstrates why Type : Type would be inconsistent in Lean.
Lean
theorem not_surjective_Type {α : Type u} (f : α → Type max u v) : ¬Surjective f := by
  intro hf
  let T : Type max u v := Sigma f
  cases hf (Set T) with | intro U hU =>
  let g : Set T → T := fun s ↦ ⟨U, cast hU.symm s⟩
  have hg : Injective g := by
    intro s t h
    suffices cast hU (g s).2 = cast hU (g t).2 by
      simp only [g, cast_cast, cast_eq] at this
      assumption
    congr
  exact cantor_injective g hg

1 lines
Function__not_surjective_Type : (X : Set) -> (f : (Elem X -> Set)) -> Proof (not (Function__Surjective f)).
English
Let $X$ be a set. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $X$ to sets. Then $f$ is not surjective.
Formula
$X \in \mathrm{Set} , f \in X \rightarrow \mathrm{Set} \vdash \neg \operatorname{Surjective} (f)$

1.To prove that we have a contradiction, assume that $f$ is surjective.intro hf
2.Let $T$ be a set.let T : Type max u v := Sigma f

4.Let $U \in X$ such that $\EqtMACRoAU {f (U)}{\wp T}$.
5.Let $g$ be a function from elements of $\wp T$ to elements of $T$.let g : Set T → T := fun s ↦ ⟨U, cast hU.symm s⟩
6.First verify that $g$ is injective.have hg : Injective g
7.Let $s , t \in \wp T$ such that $\EqtMACRoAU {g (s)}{g (t)}$.intro s t h
8.It is enough to show that $\EqtMACRoAU {\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (s)))}{\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (t)))}$.suffices cast hU (g s).2 = cast hU (g t).2 by
9.Assume that $\EqtMACRoAU {\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (s)))}{\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (t)))}$.
10.$\EqtMACRoAU {s}{t}$.simp only [g, cast_cast, cast_eq] at this
11.$\EqtMACRoAU {s}{t}$.assumption
12.$\EqtMACRoAU {\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (s)))}{\mathrm{cast} (\mathrm{hU} , \operatorname{snd} (g (t)))}$.congr

g is a partial inverse to f (an injective but not necessarily surjective function) if g y = some x implies f x = y, and g y = none implies that y is not in the range of f.

def IsPartialInv {α β} (f : α → β) (g : β → Option α) : Prop :=
  ∀ x y, g y = some x ↔ f x = y

theorem LeftInverse.eq_rightInverse {f : α → β} {g₁ g₂ : β → α} (h₁ : LeftInverse g₁ f)
    (h₂ : RightInverse g₂ f) : g₁ = g₂ :=
  calc
    g₁ = g₁ ∘ f ∘ g₂ := by rw [h₂.comp_eq_id, comp_id]
     _ = g₂ := by rw [← comp_assoc, h₁.comp_eq_id, id_comp]

Function.partialInvdefBasic.lean:490
We can use choice to construct explicitly a partial inverse for a given injective function f.
Lean
noncomputable def partialInv {α β} (f : α → β) (b : β) : Option α :=
  open scoped Classical in
  if h : ∃ a, f a = b then some (Classical.choose h) else none

  f => b => dite (exists X (a => Eqt Y (f a) b)) (h => Option__some (Classical__choose h)) (h1 => Option__none).

theorem Injective.isPartialInv {α β} {f : α → β} (I : Injective f) : IsPartialInv f (partialInv f)
  | a, b =>
  ⟨fun h =>
    open scoped Classical in
    have hpi : partialInv f b = if h : ∃ a, f a = b then some (Classical.choose h) else none :=
      rfl
    if h' : ∃ a, f a = b
    then by rw [hpi, dite_eq_left h'] at h
            injection h with h
            subst h
            apply Classical.choose_spec h'
    else by rw [hpi, dite_eq_right h'] at h; contradiction,
  fun e => e ▸ have h : ∃ a', f a' = f a := ⟨_, rfl⟩
              (dite_eq_left h).trans (congr_arg _ (I <| Classical.choose_spec h))⟩

Function.update_congrtheoremBasic.lean:657
A congruence lemma for Function.update, specialized for the non-dependent case. Without this, simp can't rewrite in the fourth argument a because the result type depends on a. See also https://github.com/leanprover/lean4/issues/12478.
Lean
@[congr]
lemma update_congr {β : Sort*}
    {f₁ f₂ : α → β} (hf : f₁ = f₂) {a'₁ a'₂ : α} (ha' : a'₁ = a'₂)
    {v₁ v₂ : β} (hv : v₁ = v₂) {a₁ a₂ : α} (ha : a₁ = a₂) :
    Function.update f₁ a'₁ v₁ a₁ = Function.update f₂ a'₂ v₂ a₂ := by
  subst hf; subst ha'; subst hv; subst ha; rfl

Function.rec_updatetheoremBasic.lean:748
Recursors can be pushed inside Function.update.
The ctor argument should be a one-argument constructor like Sum.inl, and recursor should be an inductive recursor partially applied in all but that constructor, such as (Sum.rec · g).
In future, we should build some automation to generate applications like Option.rec_update for all inductive types.
Lean

Function.extenddefBasic.lean:841
Extension of a function g : α → γ along a function f : α → β.
For every a : α, f a is sent to g a. f might not be surjective, so we use an auxiliary function j : β → γ by sending b : β not in the range of f to j b. If you do not care about the behavior outside the range, j can be used as a junk value by setting it to be 0 or Classical.arbitrary (assuming γ is nonempty).
This definition is mathematically meaningful only when f a₁ = f a₂ → g a₁ = g a₂ (spelled g.FactorsThrough f). In particular this holds if f is injective.
A typical use case is extending a function from a subtype to the entire type. If you wish to extend g : {b : β // p b} → γ to a function β → γ, you should use Function.extend Subtype.val g j.
Lean
def extend (f : α → β) (g : α → γ) (j : β → γ) : β → γ := fun b ↦
  open scoped Classical in
  if h : ∃ a, f a = b then g (Classical.choose h) else j b
Dedukti
2 lines
def Function__extend : (f : (Elem X -> Y)) -> (g : (Elem X -> Z)) -> (j : (Elem Y -> Z)) -> (x : Elem Y) -> Elem Z :=
  f => g => j => x => dite (exists X (a => Eqt Y (f a) x)) (h => g (Classical__choose h)) (h1 => j x).

Function.Injective.extend_comptheoremBasic.lean:879

theorem Injective.extend_comp {α₁ α₂ α₃ : Sort*} {f₁₂ : α₁ → α₂} (h₁₂ : Function.Injective f₁₂)
    {f₂₃ : α₂ → α₃} (h₂₃ : Function.Injective f₂₃) (g : α₁ → γ) (e' : α₃ → γ) :
    extend (f₂₃ ∘ f₁₂) g e' = extend f₂₃ (extend f₁₂ g (e' ∘ f₂₃)) e' := by
  ext a
  by_cases h₃ : ∃ b, f₂₃ b = a
  · obtain ⟨b, rfl⟩ := h₃
    rw [Injective.extend_apply h₂₃]
    by_cases h₂ : ∃ c, f₁₂ c = b
    · obtain ⟨c, rfl⟩ := h₂
      rw [h₁₂.extend_apply]
      exact (h₂₃.comp h₁₂).extend_apply _ _ _
    · rw [extend_apply' _ _ _ h₂, extend_apply', comp_apply]
      exact fun h ↦ h₂ (Exists.casesOn h fun c hc ↦ Exists.intro c (h₂₃ hc))
  · rw [extend_apply' _ _ _ h₃, extend_apply']
    exact fun h ↦ h₃ (Exists.casesOn h fun c hc ↦ Exists.intro (f₁₂ c) (hc))

  refine funext fun x ↦ ?_
  have H := congr_fun hg (f x)

  exact H

theorem surjective_comp_right_iff_injective {γ : Type*} [Nontrivial γ] :
    Surjective (fun g : β → γ ↦ g ∘ f) ↔ Injective f := by
  classical
  refine ⟨not_imp_not.mp fun not_inj surj ↦ not_subsingleton γ ⟨fun c c' ↦ ?_⟩,
    (·.surjective_comp_right)⟩
  simp only [Injective, not_forall] at not_inj
  have ⟨a₁, a₂, eq, ne⟩ := not_inj
  have ⟨f, hf⟩ := surj (if · = a₂ then c else c')
  have h₁ := congr_fun hf a₁
  have h₂ := congr_fun hf a₂
  simp only [comp_apply, ite_eq_right ne, reduceIte] at h₁ h₂
  rw [← h₁, eq, h₂]

Set.SeparatesPointsdefBasic.lean:1240
A set of functions "separates points" if for each pair of distinct points there is a function taking different values on them.
Lean
def Set.SeparatesPoints {α β : Type*} (A : Set (α → β)) : Prop :=
  ∀ ⦃x y : α⦄, x ≠ y → ∃ f ∈ A, f x ≠ f y
Dedukti
2 lines
def Set__SeparatesPoints : (A : Elem (powerset (Elem X -> Y))) -> Prop :=
  A => forall X (x => forall X (y => if (Neq x y) (exists (Elem X -> Y) (f => and (Membership__mem A f) (Neq (f x) (f y)))))).
English
Let $A \in \wp (\mathrm{Elem} (X)\rightarrow Y)$. Then $\operatorname{SeparatesPoints}$ holds for $A$, if for all elements $x$ and $y$ of $X$, if $x \neq y$, then there exists a function $f$ from elements of $X$ to elements of $Y$, such that $f$ is an element of $A$ and $f (x)\neq f (y)$.
Formula

theorem Set.separatesPoints_mono {α β : Type*} {A B : Set (α → β)} (hAB : A ⊆ B)
    (hA : Set.SeparatesPoints A) : Set.SeparatesPoints B := by
  intro x y hne
  obtain ⟨f, hfA, hne'⟩ := hA hne
  exact ⟨f, hAB hfA, hne'⟩

theorem InvImage.equivalence {α : Sort u} {β : Sort v} (r : β → β → Prop) (f : α → β)
    (h : Equivalence r) : Equivalence (InvImage r f) :=
  ⟨fun _ ↦ h.1 _, h.symm, h.trans⟩
